It enables fuzzing of Python code and Python extensions to find bugs, crashes, and security vulnerabilities.\n\n## Installation\n\n```bash\npip install atheris\n```\n\n## Basic Usage\n\n### Simple Fuzz Driver Template\n\n```python\nimport atheris\nimport sys\n\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    \"\"\"\n    Fuzz driver function that processes fuzzer-generated input.\n\n    Args:\n        data: bytes - Random input generated by the fuzzer\n    \"\"\"\n    # Convert bytes to usable format\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    # Extract data in various formats\n    string_input = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(100)\n    int_input = fdp.ConsumeInt(4)\n    bool_input = fdp.ConsumeBool()\n\n    # Test your function\n    try:\n        your_function(string_input, int_input, bool_input)\n    except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError):\n        # Expected exceptions - don't crash\n        pass\n    # Let unexpected exceptions propagate to catch bugs\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    atheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\n    atheris.Fuzz()\n```\n\n## FuzzedDataProvider Methods\n\nThe `FuzzedDataProvider` class helps consume fuzzer input in structured ways:\n\n- `ConsumeBytes(count)` - Get `count` bytes\n- `ConsumeUnicode(count)` - Get Unicode string of length `count`\n- `ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(count)` - Get valid Unicode string\n- `ConsumeString(count)` - Get ASCII string\n- `ConsumeInt(bytes)` - Get integer from `bytes` bytes\n- `ConsumeIntInRange(min, max)` - Get integer in range\n- `ConsumeFloat()` - Get float value\n- `ConsumeBool()` - Get boolean value\n- `PickValueInList(list)` - Pick item from list\n- `ConsumeIntList(count, bytes)` - Get list of integers\n- `ConsumeFloatList(count)` - Get list of floats\n\n## Running the Fuzzer\n\n```bash\n# Basic run\npython fuzz.py\n\n# Run for specific duration (10 seconds)\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10\n\n# Run with specific number of iterations\npython fuzz.py -runs=1000\n\n# Use specific corpus directory\npython fuzz.py corpus_dir/\n\n# Set memory limit (in MB)\npython fuzz.py -rss_limit_mb=2048\n\n# Print coverage information\npython fuzz.py -print_coverage=1\n```\n\n## Common Fuzzing Patterns\n\n### Testing String Parsers\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n    input_str = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(1000)\n\n    try:\n        parse_function(input_str)\n    except (ValueError, ParseError):\n        pass\n```\n\n### Testing JSON/Data Parsers\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n    json_str = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(10000)\n\n    try:\n        json.loads(json_str)\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError):\n        pass\n```\n\n### Testing Binary Parsers\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    try:\n        parse_binary_data(data)\n    except (ValueError, struct.error):\n        pass\n```\n\n### Testing with Multiple Inputs\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    # Generate multiple test inputs\n    num_items = fdp.ConsumeIntInRange(1, 10)\n    items = [fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(50) for _ in range(num_items)]\n\n    try:\n        process_items(items)\n    except ExpectedException:\n        pass\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Handle Expected Exceptions**: Catch and ignore exceptions that are expected (like ValueError for invalid input)\n2. **Let Unexpected Exceptions Crash**: Don't catch unexpected exceptions - these are bugs!\n3. **Use Appropriate Data Types**: Use `ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates()` instead of `ConsumeUnicode()` to avoid surrogate issues\n4. **Limit Input Size**: Use reasonable size limits to prevent memory exhaustion\n5. **Test Edge Cases**: Fuzz multiple code paths by using `PickValueInList()` or conditionals\n6. **Start Simple**: Begin with basic fuzzing and add complexity as needed\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Import Errors\nIf you get import errors, ensure the library is installed in the same environment as atheris:\n```bash\npython -m pip install atheris <library_name>\n```\n\n### Memory Issues\nLimit memory usage:\n```bash\npython fuzz.py -rss_limit_mb=1024\n```\n\n### Slow Execution\nReduce iterations or time:\n```bash\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=5 -timeout=1\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":157,"cache_read_input_tokens":19482,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":157,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"10232819-9bc6-4978-aee1-98f3ecacf847","uuid":"d7eaefed-e377-4ddf-b4eb-76987b3603b2"}

Python Virtual Environment Management\n\n## Overview\nVirtual environments allow you to create isolated Python environments for different projects, preventing dependency conflicts and ensuring reproducible builds.\n\n## Creating Virtual Environments\n\n### Using venv (Built-in)\n\n```bash\n# Create virtual environment\npython3 -m venv /path/to/venv\n\n# or in current directory\npython3 -m venv .venv\n```\n\n### Using virtualenv (Third-party)\n\n```bash\n# Install virtualenv\npip install virtualenv\n\n# Create virtual environment\nvirtualenv /path/to/venv\n```\n\n## Activating Virtual Environments\n\n### Linux/Mac\n```bash\nsource /path/to/venv/bin/activate\n```\n\n### Windows\n```bash\n.\\venv\\Scripts\\activate\n```\n\n## Running Commands in Virtual Environment Without Activation\n\nInstead of activating, you can directly use the virtual environment's Python:\n\n```bash\n# Run Python\n/path/to/venv/bin/python script.py\n\n# Run pip\n/path/to/venv/bin/pip install package\n\n# Run installed commands\n/path/to/venv/bin/command\n```\n\n## Installing Dependencies\n\n### From requirements.txt\n```bash\n# After activation\npip install -r requirements.txt\n\n# Without activation\n/path/to/venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n### From pyproject.toml (Modern Python Projects)\n\n```bash\n# Install project in editable mode with dependencies\npip install -e .\n\n# Without activation\n/path/to/venv/bin/pip install -e .\n\n# Install with specific extras\npip install -e \".[dev,test]\"\n```\n\n### From setup.py (Legacy)\n\n```bash\n# Install project\npip install .\n\n# Install in editable/development mode\npip install -e .\n```\n\n## Common Dependency File Formats\n\n### requirements.txt\n```\npackage1==1.0.0\npackage2>=2.0.0\npackage3\n```\n\n### pyproject.toml (PEP 621)\n```toml\n[project]\ndependencies = [\n    \"package1>=1.0.0\",\n    \"package2\",\n]\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest\", \"black\"]\ntest = [\"pytest-cov\"]\n```\n\n### setup.py\n```python\nsetup(\n    name=\"mypackage\",\n    install_requires=[\n        \"package1>=1.0.0\",\n        \"package2\",\n    ],\n)\n```\n\n## Useful Commands\n\n```bash\n# List installed packages\npip list\n\n# Freeze current environment to requirements.txt\npip freeze > requirements.txt\n\n# Show package information\npip show package_name\n\n# Uninstall package\npip uninstall package_name\n\n# Update pip itself\npip install --upgrade pip\n```\n\n## Deactivating Virtual Environment\n\n```bash\ndeactivate\n```\n\n## Removing Virtual Environment\n\n```bash\n# Simply delete the directory\nrm -rf /path/to/venv\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Always use virtual environments** - Never install packages globally for projects\n2. **Name consistently** - Use `.venv` or `venv` as standard names\n3. **Add to .gitignore** - Don't commit virtual environments to version control\n4. **Document dependencies** - Keep requirements.txt or pyproject.toml updated\n5. **Use specific versions** - Pin versions for reproducibility in production\n6. **Separate dev dependencies** - Use optional dependencies or separate requirements files\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### Quick Setup Script\n```bash\n#!/bin/bash\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip reproducible builds.\n\n## Creating Virtual Environments\n\n### Using venv (Built-in)\n\n```bash\n# Create virtual environment\npython3 -m venv /path/to/venv\n\n# or in current directory\npython3 -m venv .venv\n```\n\n### Using virtualenv (Third-party)\n\n```bash\n# Install virtualenv\npip install virtualenv\n\n# Create virtual environment\nvirtualenv /path/to/venv\n```\n\n## Activating Virtual Environments\n\n### Linux/Mac\n```bash\nsource /path/to/venv/bin/activate\n```\n\n### Windows\n```bash\n.\\venv\\Scripts\\activate\n```\n\n## Running Commands in Virtual Environment Without Activation\n\nInstead of activating, you can directly use the virtual environment's Python:\n\n```bash\n# Run Python\n/path/to/venv/bin/python script.py\n\n# Run pip\n/path/to/venv/bin/pip install package\n\n# Run installed commands\n/path/to/venv/bin/command\n```\n\n## Installing Dependencies\n\n### From requirements.txt\n```bash\n# After activation\npip install -r requirements.txt\n\n# Without activation\n/path/to/venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n### From pyproject.toml (Modern Python Projects)\n\n```bash\n# Install project in editable mode with dependencies\npip install -e .\n\n# Without activation\n/path/to/venv/bin/pip install -e .\n\n# Install with specific extras\npip install -e \".[dev,test]\"\n```\n\n### From setup.py (Legacy)\n\n```bash\n# Install project\npip install .\n\n# Install in editable/development mode\npip install -e .\n```\n\n## Common Dependency File Formats\n\n### \"package2\",\n]\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest\", \"black\"]\ntest = [\"pytest-cov\"]\n```\n\n### setup.py\n```python\nsetup(\n    name=\"mypackage\",\n    install_requires=[\n        \"package1>=1.0.0\",\n        \"package2\",\n    ],\n)\n```\n\n## Useful Commands\n\n```bash\n# List installed packages\npip list\n\n# Freeze current environment to requirements.txt\npip freeze > requirements.txt\n\n# Show package information\npip show package_name\n\n# Uninstall package\npip uninstall package_name\n\n# Update pip itself\npip install --upgrade pip\n```\n\n## Deactivating Virtual Environment\n\n```bash\ndeactivate\n```\n\n## Removing Virtual Environment\n\n```bash\n# Simply delete the directory\nrm -rf /path/to/venv\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Always use virtual environments** - Never install packages globally for projects\n2. **Name consistently** - Use `.venv` or `venv` as standard names\n3. **Add to .gitignore** - Don't commit virtual environments to version control\n4. **Document dependencies** - Keep requirements.txt or pyproject.toml updated\n5. **Use specific versions** - Pin versions for reproducibility in production\n6. **Separate dev dependencies** - Use optional dependencies or separate requirements files\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### Quick Setup Script\n```bash\n#!/bin/bash\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip This guide covers techniques for library analysis.\n\n## Understanding Library Structure\n\n### 1. Check Project Files\n\nLook for these files to understand the project:\n- `README.md` - Project description and usage examples\n- `pyproject.toml` - Modern Python project configuration\n- `setup.py` - Legacy project configuration\n- `requirements.txt` - Dependencies\n- `docs/` - Documentation directory\n- `tests/` - Test suite (great for understanding usage)\n\n### 2. Identify Main Module\n\n```bash\n# Find the main package directory\nls -la | grep -E \"^d\"\n\n# Look for __init__.py files\nfind . -name \"__init__.py\" -type f\n\n# Check pyproject.toml or setup.py for package name\ngrep -E \"name|packages\" pyproject.toml setup.py\n```\n\n## Finding Functions to Fuzz\n\n### Priority Functions (High Value Targets)\n\n1. **Parsers and Deserializers**\n   - JSON, XML, YAML parsers\n   - Binary format parsers\n   - Custom data format parsers\n   - String parsing functions\n\n2. **Input Processing Functions**\n   - Functions that accept user input\n   - URL/path processing\n   - File reading/processing\n   - Configuration parsing\n\n3. **Data Transformation**\n   - Encoding/decoding functions\n   - Format converters\n   - Data validators\n   - String manipulation\n\n4. **Public API Functions**\n   - Main entry points\n   - Commonly used functions\n   - Functions with complex logic\n\n### Analysis Techniques\n\n#### Read the Main Module\n\n```python\n# Import and inspect\nimport library_name\nimport inspect\n\n# List all public functions\npublic_functions = [name for name in dir(library_name) if not name.startswith('_')]\n\n# Get function signatures\nfor func_name in public_functions:\n    func = getattr(library_name, func_name)\n    if callable(func):\n        sig = inspect.signature(func)\n        print(f\"{func_name}{sig}\")\n```\n\n#### Examine Test Suite\n\nTests show how functions are used:\n```bash\n# Find test files\nfind . -path \"*/test*\" -name \"*.py\"\n\n# Look for common test patterns\ngrep -r \"def test_\" tests/\ngrep -r \"assert\" tests/\n```\n\n#### Check Documentation\n\n```bash\n# Find documentation\nfind . -name \"*.md\" -o -name \"*.rst\"\n\n# Look for API documentation\nls docs/api* docs/reference*\n```\n\n#### Analyze Common Patterns\n\n```bash\n# Find string-accepting functions (good fuzz targets)\ngrep -r \"def.*str\" --include=\"*.py\" | head -20\n\n# Find functions with multiple parameters\ngrep -r \"def.*,.*,.*:\" --include=\"*.py\" | head -20\n\n# Find exception handling (indicates error-prone areas)\ngrep -r \"except\\|raise\" --include=\"*.py\" | wc -l\n```\n\n## Example Analysis Workflow\n\n### For a JSON Library (like ujson)\n\n1. **Identify main functions**:\n   - `loads()` - Parse JSON string\n   - `dumps()` - Serialize to JSON\n   - `load()` - Parse JSON file\n   - `dump()` - Serialize to JSON file\n\n2. **Determine input types**:\n   - `loads()` takes string/bytes\n   - `dumps()` takes Python objects\n   - Both have optional parameters\n\n3. **Find edge cases from tests**:\n   - Large numbers\n   - Unicode strings\n   - Nested structures\n   - Invalid JSON\n\n### For a Date/Time Library (like arrow)\n\n1. **Identify main functions**:\n   - `get()` - Parse datetime\n   - `now()` - Current time\n   - `Arrow.format()` - Format datetime\n   - `Arrow.shift()` - Modify datetime\n\n2. **Input types**:\n   - String parsing\n   - Timestamp integers\n   - Format strings\n   - Timezone strings\n\n### For a Code Formatter (like black)\n\n1. **Main functions**:\n   - `format_str()` - Format Python code\n   - `format_file_contents()` - Format file\n   - Configuration parsing\n\n2. **Input types**:\n   - Python source code strings\n   - Configuration dictionaries\n   - File paths\n\n## Creating Testing Notes\n\n### Template Structure\n\n```markdown\n# Library Name - Testing Notes\n\n## Library Purpose\n[Brief description of what the library does]\n\n## Main Module/Package\n- Import path: `import library.module`\n- Entry point: `library.main_function()`\n\n## High-Priority Functions for Fuzzing\n\n### 1. function_name()\n- **Location**: `module.submodule.function_name`\n- **Purpose**: [What it does]\n- **Input types**: [str, int, dict, etc.]\n- **Edge cases**: [Known problematic inputs]\n- **Exceptions**: [What exceptions it raises]\n\n### 2. another_function()\n- **Location**: `module.another_function`\n- **Purpose**: [What it does]\n- ...\n\n## Dependencies\n- List key dependencies that need to be installed\n\n## Known Issues\n- Any known bugs or limitations\n\n## Testing Strategy\n- How to structure fuzz tests\n- What to focus on\n```\n\n## Quick Analysis Commands\n\n```bash\n# Count lines of code (complexity indicator)\nfind . -name \"*.py\" -type f -exec wc -l {} + | sort -n\n\n# Find most complex files (likely have bugs)\nfind . -name \"*.py\" -exec grep -c \"if\\|for\\|while\\|try\" {} + | sort -t: -k2 -n\n\n# Find functions with many parameters (complex APIs)\ngrep -rn \"^def.*,.*,.*,.*:\" --include=\"*.py\"\n\n# Find all imports (understand dependencies)\ngrep -rh \"^import\\|^from\" --include=\"*.py\" | sort -u\n```\n\n## Red Flags (Good Fuzz Targets)\n\nLook for:\n- `eval()` or `exec()` calls\n- String formatting with user input\n- Complex parsing logic\n- Recursive functions\n- Manual memory management (C extensions)\n- This guide covers how to identify and install dependencies for any Python project.\n\n## Dependency File Types\n\n### 1. pyproject.toml (Modern Standard - PEP 518/621)\n\n**Location**: Root of project\n\n**Content Example**:\n```toml\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"setuptools>=40.8.0\", \"wheel\"]\n\n[project]\nname = \"myproject\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"requests>=2.28.0\",\n    \"click>=8.0\",\n]\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest>=7.0\", \"black>=22.0\"]\ntest = [\"pytest-cov>=3.0\"]\n```\n\n**Installation**:\n```bash\n# Install project with dependencies\npip install .\n\n# Install in editable mode (for development)\npip install -e .\n\n# Install with optional dependencies\npip install -e \".[dev]\"\npip install -e \".[dev,test]\"\n```\n\n### 2. setup.py (Legacy Standard)\n\n**Location**: Root of project\n\n**Content Example**:\n```python\nfrom setuptools import setup\n\nsetup(\n    name=\"myproject\",\n    install_requires=[\n        \"requests>=2.28.0\",\n        \"click>=8.0\",\n    ],\n    extras_require={\n        \"dev\": [\"pytest>=7.0\", \"black>=22.0\"],\n    },\n)\n```\n\n**Installation**:\n```bash\n# Install project with dependencies\npip install .\n\n# Install in editable mode\npip install -e .\n\n# Install with extras\npip install -e \".[dev]\"\n```\n\n### 3. setup.cfg (Alternative Configuration)\n\n**Location**: Root of project\n\n**Content Example**:\n```ini\n[metadata]\nname = myproject\n\n[options]\ninstall_requires =\n    requests>=2.28.0\n    click>=8.0\n\n[options.extras_require]\ndev =\n    pytest>=7.0\n    black>=22.0\n```\n\n**Installation**: Same as setup.py\n\n### 4. requirements.txt (Simple Dependency List)\n\n**Location**: Root or requirements/ directory\n\n**Content Example**:\n```\nrequests==2.28.1\nclick>=8.0.0\nnumpy~=1.23.0\n```\n\n**Installation**:\n```bash\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n**Common Variations**:\n- `requirements.txt` - Main dependencies\n- `requirements-dev.txt` - Development dependencies\n- `requirements-test.txt` - Testing dependencies\n- `requirements/base.txt` - Base dependencies\n- `requirements/prod.txt` - Production dependencies\n\n### 5. Pipfile (Pipenv)\n\n**Location**: Root of project\n\n**Content Example**:\n```toml\n[packages]\nrequests = \">=2.28.0\"\nclick = \"*\"\n\n[dev-packages]\npytest = \"*\"\n```\n\n**Installation**:\n```bash\npipenv install\npipenv install --dev  # Include dev dependencies\n```\n\n### 6. poetry.lock / pyproject.toml (Poetry)\n\n**Installation**:\n```bash\npoetry install\npoetry install --no-dev  # Exclude dev dependencies\n```\n\n## Detection and Installation Strategy\n\n### Automatic Detection Script\n\n```bash\n#!/bin/bash\n# install_dependencies.sh\n\nif [ -f \"pyproject.toml\" ]; then\n    echo \"Found pyproject.toml\"\n    if grep -q \"\\[tool.poetry\\]\" pyproject.toml; then\n        echo \"Installing with Poetry\"\n        poetry install\n    else\n        echo \"Installing with pip\"\n        pip install -e .\n    fi\nelif [ -f \"setup.py\" ]; then\n    echo \"Found setup.py, installing with pip\"\n    pip install -e .\nelif [ -f \"requirements.txt\" ]; then\n    echo \"Found requirements.txt\"\n    pip install -r requirements.txt\nelif [ -f \"Pipfile\" ]; then\n    echo \"Found Pipfile, installing with pipenv\"\n    pipenv install\nelse\n    echo \"No dependency file found\"\nfi\n```\n\n### Comprehensive Installation Command\n\n```bash\n# Try multiple approaches in order of preference\nif [ -f \"pyproject.toml\" ]; then\n    pip install -e . || pip install .\nelif [ -f \"setup.py\" ]; then\n    pip install -e . || pip install .\nfi\n\n# Also install from requirements.txt if it exists\nif [ -f \"requirements.txt\" ]; then\n    pip install -r requirements.txt\nfi\n\n# Install common dev dependencies\nif [ -f \"requirements-dev.txt\" ]; then\n    pip install -r requirements-dev.txt\nfi\n```\n\n## Common Installation Patterns\n\n### Pattern 1: Modern Project (pyproject.toml only)\n```bash\ncd /path/to/project\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install --upgrade pip\npip install -e .\n```\n\n### Pattern 2: Legacy Project (setup.py)\n```bash\ncd /path/to/project\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install --upgrade pip\npip install -e .\n```\n\n### Pattern 3: Requirements File Only\n```bash\ncd /path/to/project\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install --upgrade pip\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n### Pattern 4: Editable Install with Development Dependencies\n```bash\ncd /path/to/project\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install --upgrade pip\npip install -e \".[dev,test]\"  # For pyproject.toml/setup.py\n# OR\npip install -r requirements-dev.txt  # For requirements files\n```\n\n## Reading Dependencies Programmatically\n\n### From pyproject.toml\n```python\nimport toml\n\nwith open(\"pyproject.toml\") as f:\n    config = toml.load(f)\n\n# PEP 621 format\nif \"project\" in config and \"dependencies\" in config[\"project\"]:\n    deps = config[\"project\"][\"dependencies\"]\n\n# Poetry format\nif \"tool\" in config and \"poetry\" in config[\"tool\"]:\n    deps = config[\"tool\"][\"poetry\"][\"dependencies\"]\n```\n\n### From setup.py (Risky - may execute code)\n```bash\n# Safer: grep for install_requires\ngrep -A 20 \"install_requires\" setup.py\n```\n\n### From requirements.txt\n```bash\n# Just read the file\ncat requirements.txt\n\n# Filter out comments and empty lines\ngrep -v \"^#\" requirements.txt | grep -v \"^$\"\n```\n\n## Installing Additional Dependencies for Fuzzing\n\n```bash\n# Install the project\npip install -e .\n\n# Install fuzzing tools\npip install atheris\n\n# Install any additional testing tools\npip install pytest pytest-cov\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Missing Build Dependencies\n```bash\n# Error: \"ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'setuptools'\"\npip install setuptools wheel\n\n# Error: No module named 'Cython'\npip install Cython\n```\n\n### Compilation Errors (C extensions)\n```bash\n# Install build tools (Ubuntu/Debian)\napt-get install python3-dev build-essential\n\n# Install build tools (macOS)\nxcode-select --install\n```\n\n### Version Conflicts\n```bash\n# Force reinstall\npip install --force-reinstall package_name\n\n# Ignore installed packages\npip install --ignore-installed package_name\n```\n\n### Permission Errors\n```bash\n# Use virtual environment (recommended)\npython3 -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate\n\n# Or use --user flag (not recommended)\npip install --user package_name\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Always use virtual environments** - Isolate project dependencies\n2. **Upgrade pip first** - `pip install --upgrade pip`\n3. **Use editable installs for development** - `pip install -e .`\n4. **Check for multiple dependency files** - Install from all relevant files\n5. **Read build-system requirements** - Some projects need specific build tools\n6. **Handle C extensions** - Ensure system This guide covers how to identify and install dependencies for any Python project.\n\n## Dependency File Types\n\n### 1. pyproject.toml (Modern Standard - PEP 518/621)\n\n**Location**: Root of project\n\n**Content Example**:\n```toml\n[build-system]\nrequires = [\"setuptools>=40.8.0\", \"wheel\"]\n\n[project]\nname = \"myproject\"\ndependencies = [\n    \"requests>=2.28.0\",\n    \"click>=8.0\",\n]\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest>=7.0\", \"black>=22.0\"]\ntest = [\"pytest-cov>=3.0\"]\n```\n\n**Installation**:\n```bash\n# Install project with dependencies\npip install .\n\n# Install in editable mode (for development)\npip install -e .\n\n# Install with optional dependencies\npip install -e \".[dev]\"\npip install -e \".[dev,test]\"\n```\n\n### 2. setup.py (Legacy Standard)\n\n**Location**: Root of project\n\n**Content Example**:\n```python\nfrom setuptools import setup\n\nsetup(\n    name=\"myproject\",\n    install_requires=[\n        \"requests>=2.28.0\",\n        \"click>=8.0\",\n    ],\n    extras_require={\n        \"dev\": [\"pytest>=7.0\", \"black>=22.0\"],\n    },\n)\n```\n\n**Installation**:\n```bash\n# Install project with dependencies\npip install .\n\n# Install in editable mode\npip install -e .\n\n# Install with extras\npip install -e \".[dev]\"\n```\n\n### 3. setup.cfg (Alternative Configuration)\n\n**Location**: Root of project\n\n**Content Example**:\n```ini\n[metadata]\nname = myproject\n\n[options]\ninstall_requires =\n    requests>=2.28.0\n    click>=8.0\n\n[options.extras_require]\ndev =\n    pytest>=7.0\n    black>=22.0\n```\n\n**Installation**: Same as setup.py\n\n### 4. requirements.txt (Simple Dependency List)\n\n**Location**: Root or requirements/ directory\n\n**Content Example**:\n```\nrequests==2.28.1\nclick>=8.0.0\nnumpy~=1.23.0\n```\n\n**Installation**:\n```bash\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n**Common Variations**:\n- `requirements.txt` - Main dependencies\n- `requirements-dev.txt` - Development dependencies\n- `requirements-test.txt` - Testing dependencies\n- `requirements/base.txt` - Base dependencies\n- `requirements/prod.txt` - Production dependencies\n\n### 5. Pipfile (Pipenv)\n\n**Location**: Root of project\n\n**Content Example**:\n```toml\n[packages]\nrequests = \">=2.28.0\"\nclick = \"*\"\n\n[dev-packages]\npytest = \"*\"\n```\n\n**Installation**:\n```bash\npipenv install\npipenv install --dev  # Include dev dependencies\n```\n\n### 6. poetry.lock / pyproject.toml (Poetry)\n\n**Installation**:\n```bash\npoetry install\npoetry install --no-dev  # Exclude dev dependencies\n```\n\n## Detection and Installation Strategy\n\n### Automatic Detection Script\n\n```bash\n#!/bin/bash\n# install_dependencies.sh\n\nif [ -f \"pyproject.toml\" ]; then\n    echo \"Found pyproject.toml\"\n    if grep -q \"\\[tool.poetry\\]\" pyproject.toml; then\n        echo \"Installing with Poetry\"\n        poetry install\n    else\n        echo \"Installing with pip\"\n        pip install -e .\n    fi\nelif [ -f \"setup.py\" ]; then\n    echo \"Found setup.py, installing with pip\"\n    pip install -e .\nelif [ -f \"requirements.txt\" ]; then\n    echo \"Found requirements.txt\"\n    pip install -r requirements.txt\nelif [ -f \"Pipfile\" ]; then\n    echo \"Found Pipfile, installing with pipenv\"\n    pipenv install\nelse\n    echo \"No dependency file found\"\nfi\n```\n\n### Comprehensive Installation Command\n\n```bash\n# Try multiple approaches in order of preference\nif [ -f \"pyproject.toml\" ]; then\n    pip install -e . || pip install .\nelif [ -f \"setup.py\" ]; then\n    pip install -e . || pip install .\nfi\n\n# Also install from requirements.txt if it exists\nif [ -f \"requirements.txt\" ]; then\n    pip install -r requirements.txt\nfi\n\n# Install common dev dependencies\nif [ -f \"requirements-dev.txt\" ]; then\n    pip install -r requirements-dev.txt\nfi\n```\n\n## Common Installation Patterns\n\n### Pattern 1: Modern Project (pyproject.toml only)\n```bash\ncd /path/to/project\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install --upgrade pip\npip install -e .\n```\n\n### Pattern 2: Legacy Project (setup.py)\n```bash\ncd /path/to/project\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install --upgrade pip\npip install -e .\n```\n\n### Pattern 3: Requirements File Only\n```bash\ncd /path/to/project\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install --upgrade pip\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n### Pattern 4: Editable Install with Development Dependencies\n```bash\ncd /path/to/project\npython3 -m venv .venv\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install --upgrade pip\npip install -e \".[dev,test]\"  # For pyproject.toml/setup.py\n# OR\npip install -r requirements-dev.txt  # For requirements files\n```\n\n## Reading Dependencies Programmatically\n\n### From pyproject.toml\n```python\nimport toml\n\nwith open(\"pyproject.toml\") as f:\n    config = toml.load(f)\n\n# PEP 621 format\nif \"project\" in config and \"dependencies\" in config[\"project\"]:\n    deps = config[\"project\"][\"dependencies\"]\n\n# Poetry format\nif \"tool\" in config and \"poetry\" in config[\"tool\"]:\n    deps = config[\"tool\"][\"poetry\"][\"dependencies\"]\n```\n\n### From setup.py (Risky - may execute code)\n```bash\n# Safer: grep for install_requires\ngrep -A 20 \"install_requires\" setup.py\n```\n\n### From requirements.txt\n```bash\n# Just read the file\ncat requirements.txt\n\n# Filter out comments and empty lines\ngrep -v \"^#\" requirements.txt | grep -v \"^$\"\n```\n\n## Installing Additional Dependencies for Fuzzing\n\n```bash\n# Install the project\npip install -e .\n\n# Install fuzzing tools\npip install atheris\n\n# Install any additional testing tools\npip install pytest pytest-cov\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Missing Build Dependencies\n```bash\n# Error: \"ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'setuptools'\"\npip install setuptools wheel\n\n# Error: No module named 'Cython'\npip install Cython\n```\n\n### Compilation Errors (C extensions)\n```bash\n# Install build tools (Ubuntu/Debian)\napt-get install python3-dev build-essential\n\n# Install build tools (macOS)\nxcode-select --install\n```\n\n### Version Conflicts\n```bash\n# Force reinstall\npip install --force-reinstall package_name\n\n# Ignore installed packages\npip install --ignore-installed package_name\n```\n\n### Permission Errors\n```bash\n# Use virtual environment (recommended)\npython3 -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate\n\n# Or use --user flag (not recommended)\npip install --user package_name\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Always use virtual environments** - Isolate project dependencies\n2. **Upgrade pip first** - `pip install --upgrade pip`\n3. **Use editable installs for development** - `pip install -e .`\n4. **Check for multiple dependency files** - Install from all relevant files\n5. **Read build-system requirements** - Some projects need specific build tools\n6. **Handle C extensions** - Ensure system {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_014hGSjk7NBrvttcMUxm53gx","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_0137aupUhHmBFvHyr2aqsJFW","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/environment/skills/coverage-guided-fuzzing-concepts.md","content":"# Coverage-Guided Fuzzing Concepts\n\n## Overview\nCoverage-guided fuzzing (also called feedback-driven or greybox fuzzing) is an automated testing technique that uses code coverage information to guide test input generation. It's highly effective at finding bugs, crashes, and security vulnerabilities.\n\n## How Coverage-Guided Fuzzing Works\n\n### Basic Workflow\n\n1. **Initial Input**: Start with seed inputs (can be empty)\n2. **Execute**: Run the target function with the input\n3. **Measure Coverage**: Track which code paths were executed\n4. **Mutate**: Modify inputs that discovered new coverage\n5. **Repeat**: Continue the cycle to explore more code paths\n\n### Key Concepts\n\n#### Code Coverage\n- **Edge Coverage**: Tracks transitions between basic blocks\n- **Path Coverage**: Tracks sequences of edges\n- **Branch Coverage**: Tracks which branches (if/else) are taken\n\nThe fuzzer prioritizes inputs that increase coverage, focusing on unexplored code.\n\n#### Input Mutation\nFuzzers mutate inputs in various ways:\n- **Bit flipping**: Toggle random bits\n- **Byte insertion/deletion**: Add or remove bytes\n- **Arithmetic mutations**: Increment/decrement values\n- **Block operations**: Duplicate, swap, or delete chunks\n- **Dictionary-based**: Insert known interesting values (e.g., \"0\", \"-1\", \"null\")\n\n#### Corpus\n- **Corpus**: Collection of interesting inputs that discovered new coverage\n- **Minimization**: Keep only minimal inputs that maintain coverage\n- **Seed Corpus**: Initial inputs to start fuzzing\n\n## Fuzzing Targets\n\n### Good Targets\n1. **Parsers**: JSON, XML, HTML, binary formats\n2. **Decoders**: Image, video, audio, compression\n3. **Interpreters**: Scripts, expressions, configuration\n4. **Serializers**: Data serialization/deserialization\n5. **Network Protocol Handlers**: HTTP, DNS, custom protocols\n6. **File Format Handlers**: Document readers, archive extractors\n7. **Cryptographic Functions**: Hashing, encryption (for crashes, not crypto bugs)\n8. **String Processing**: Regular expressions, formatting\n\n### Poor Targets\n- Pure mathematical functions (deterministic, no parsing)\n- Functions with no branches (linear code)\n- Functions that require specific authentication\n- Functions with environmental dependencies (databases, network)\n\n## Writing Effective Fuzz Harnesses\n\n### Harness Design Principles\n\n1. **Fast Execution**: Remove I/O, network calls, sleeps\n2. **Deterministic**: Same input = same behavior\n3. **Isolated**: No global state, no side effects\n4. **Focused**: Test one function/component at a time\n\n### Example: Bad Harness\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    # BAD: Writing to disk (slow I/O)\n    with open('/tmp/fuzz_input', 'wb') as f:\n        f.write(data)\n\n    # BAD: Non-deterministic (uses current time)\n    timestamp = time.time()\n\n    # BAD: External dependency\n    result = requests.get('http://api.example.com', data=data)\n```\n\n### Example: Good Harness\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n    input_str = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(1000)\n\n    try:\n        # GOOD: Pure function, fast, deterministic\n        parse_function(input_str)\n    except ExpectedException:\n        # GOOD: Ignore expected exceptions\n        pass\n    # GOOD: Let unexpected exceptions crash (bugs!)\n```\n\n## Exception Handling Strategy\n\n### Expected vs Unexpected Exceptions\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n    input_str = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(100)\n\n    try:\n        result = parse_json(input_str)\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError, TypeError):\n        # Expected: Invalid input should raise these\n        pass\n    except KeyError:\n        # Expected: Missing keys are OK\n        pass\n    # AssertionError, MemoryError, etc. will crash - these are BUGS!\n```\n\n### What Should Crash?\n\n**Let these crash** (they indicate bugs):\n- `AssertionError`: Violated invariant\n- `MemoryError`: Memory leak or excessive allocation\n- `RecursionError`: Stack overflow\n- `SystemError`: Internal Python error\n- `AttributeError`: Unexpected attribute access\n- `IndexError`: Unexpected index access (in library code)\n- Segmentation faults: Memory corruption\n- Any unhandled exception type\n\n**Catch these** (they're expected for invalid input):\n- `ValueError`: Invalid value\n- `TypeError`: Wrong type\n- `KeyError`: Missing dictionary key (if expected)\n- `json.JSONDecodeError`: Invalid JSON\n- Custom exceptions for invalid input\n\n## Fuzzing Duration and Metrics\n\n### How Long to Fuzz?\n\n- **Quick validation**: 10-60 seconds (basic smoke test)\n- **Development**: 5-30 minutes (find obvious bugs)\n- **CI/CD**: 1-10 minutes per test (regression testing)\n- **Continuous fuzzing**: Hours to days (deep bug finding)\n\n### Success Metrics\n\n1. **Coverage**: Percentage of code reached\n   - Aim for >80% coverage of target function\n2. **Executions per second**: Higher is better\n   - Good: >1000 exec/s\n   - Acceptable: >100 exec/s\n   - Slow: <100 exec/s (may need optimization)\n3. **Bugs found**: Crashes, hangs, assertion failures\n4. **Corpus size**: Number of unique inputs maintained\n\n## Common Fuzzing Issues\n\n### Issue: Slow Fuzzing (Low exec/s)\n\n**Causes**:\n- I/O operations (file, network)\n- Complex computations\n- Poor harness design\n\n**Solutions**:\n- Remove I/O, mock external dependencies\n- Use smaller input sizes\n- Profile and optimize harness\n\n### Issue: No Coverage Growth\n\n**Causes**:\n- Input validation filters out fuzzer inputs\n- Cryptographic checksums\n- Magic bytes/headers required\n\n**Solutions**:\n- Provide seed corpus with valid inputs\n- Bypass validation in harness\n- Use custom mutators for structured input\n\n### Issue: Immediate Crashes\n\n**Causes**:\n- Real bug found (good!)\n- Poor exception handling\n- Missing dependencies\n\n**Solutions**:\n- Investigate crash (fix bug or add exception handling)\n- Ensure environment is set up correctly\n\n## Fuzzing Best Practices\n\n1. **Start Simple**: Begin with basic harness, add complexity gradually\n2. **Use Seed Corpus**: Provide valid examples for structured formats\n3. **Handle Expected Exceptions**: Catch only exceptions for invalid input\n4. **Monitor Coverage**: Ensure new code paths are explored\n5. **Minimize Input Size**: Use reasonable limits (100-10000 bytes typically)\n6. **Run Regularly**: Integrate into CI/CD for regression testing\n7. **Investigate Crashes**: Every crash is a potential bug\n8. **Share Findings**: Report bugs to maintainers\n\n## LibFuzzer-Specific Options\n\nCommon command-line options:\n\n```bash\n# Time limits\n-max_total_time=60  # Run for 60 seconds\n-timeout=1          # Per-input timeout (seconds)\n\n# Iteration limits\n-runs=1000          # Run 1000 iterations then exit\n\n# Memory limits\n-rss_limit_mb=2048  # Limit memory to 2GB\n\n# Corpus management\n-merge=1            # Merge and minimize corpus\ncorpus_dir/         # Use/save corpus to directory\n\n# Output control\n-verbosity=1        # Control output detail (0-3)\n-print_coverage=1   # Print coverage info\n-print_final_stats=1  # Print statistics at end\n\n# Debugging\n-help=1             # Show all options\n-runs=1             # Single run for debugging\n```\n\n## Example: Complete Fuzzing Workflow\n\n```python\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"\nFuzz harness for MyLibrary JSON parser\n\"\"\"\nimport sys\nimport atheris\nimport mylib\n\n\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    \"\"\"Fuzz the JSON parser\"\"\"\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    # Generate test input\n    json_str = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(10000)\n\n    try:\n        # Test parsing\n        result = mylib.parse_json(json_str)\n\n        # Test further processing if parse succeeds\n        if result:\n            mylib.validate(result)\n            mylib.transform(result)\n\n    except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError, mylib.ParseError):\n        # Expected exceptions for invalid input\n        pass\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    atheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\n    atheris.Fuzz()\n```\n\nRun it:\n```bash\n# Quick validation (10 seconds)\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10\n\n# Longer run with corpus\nmkdir corpus\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=300 corpus/\n\n# View final statistics\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=60 -print_final_stats=1\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":3,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2206,"cache_read_input_tokens":23787,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":2206,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":2},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"10232819-9bc6-4978-aee1-98f3ecacf847","uuid":"ac95d654-f314-452c-a321-8c3d99b4dde0"}

It automatically formats Python code to conform to PEP 8 style guide, with opinionated style choices.\n\n## Main Module/Package\n- Import path: `import black`\n- Main entry points: `black.format_str()`, `black.format_file_contents()`\n- Key classes: `Mode`, parsing functions\n\n## High-Priority Functions for Fuzzing\n\n### 1. format_str()\n- **Location**: `black.__init__.format_str` (line ~1177)\n- **Purpose**: Format a Python source code string\n- **Input types**: str (Python source code), Mode object (configuration)\n- **Edge cases**:\n  - Mixed quote types\n  - Escaped quotes\n  - Triple-quoted strings\n  - Raw strings, f-strings\n- **Exceptions**: Various string processing errors\n- **Why fuzz**: Complex string processing logic\n\n## Dependencies\n- click (CLI framework)\n- mypy-extensions\n- pathspec\n- platformdirs\n- tomli (for Python <3.11)\n- typing-extensions\n\n## Known Issues\n- Parsing edge cases in Python syntax\n- String processing can be complex\n- AST manipulation can have bugs\n- Performance issues with very large files\n\n## Testing Strategy\n1. With a compact codebase of **~5,000 lines of Python**, it serves as both a capable inference engine and a transparent reference for researchers and developers.\n\n## ✨ Key Features\n\n- **High Performance**: Achieves state-of-the-art throughput and latency with advanced optimizations.\n- **Lightweight & Readable**: A clean, modular, and fully type-annotated codebase that is easy to understand and modify.\n- **Advanced Optimizations**:\n  - **Radix Cache**: Reuses KV cache for shared prefixes across requests.\n  - **Chunked Prefill**: Reduces peak memory usage for long-context serving.\n  - **Overlap Scheduling**: Hides CPU scheduling overhead with GPU computation.\n  - **Tensor Parallelism**: Scales inference across multiple GPUs.\n  - **Optimized Kernels**: Integrates **FlashAttention** and **FlashInfer** for maximum efficiency.\n  - ...\n\n## 🚀 Quick Start\n\n> **⚠️ Platform Support**: Mini-SGLang currently supports **Linux only** (x86_64 and aarch64). Windows and macOS are not supported due to dependencies on Linux-specific CUDA kernels (`sgl-kernel`, `flashinfer`). We recommend using [WSL2](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/wsl/install) on Windows or Docker for cross-platform compatibility.\n\n### 1. Environment Setup\n\nWe recommend using `uv` for a fast and reliable installation (note that `uv` does not conflict with `conda`).\n\n```bash\n# Create a virtual environment (Python 3.10+ recommended)\nuv venv --python=3.12\nsource .venv/bin/activate\n```\n\n**Prerequisites**: Mini-SGLang relies on CUDA kernels that are JIT-compiled. Default is `False`:\n    50→\n    51→```pycon\n    52→>>> ujson.dumps(\"<script>John&Doe\", encode_html_chars=True)\n    53→'\"\\\\u003cscript\\\\u003eJohn\\\\u0026Doe\"'\n    54→```\n    55→\n    56→#### ensure_ascii\n    57→\n    58→Limits output to ASCII and escapes all extended characters above 127. Users are encouraged to migrate\n> to [orjson](https://pypi.org/project/orjson/) which is both much faster and\n> less likely to introduce a surprise buffer overflow vulnerability in the\n> future.\n\n## Usage\n\nMay be used as a drop in replacement for most other JSON parsers for Python:\n\n```pycon\n>>> import ujson\n>>> ujson.dumps([{\"key\": \"value\"}, 81, True])\n'[{\"key\":\"value\"},81,true]'\n>>> ujson.loads(\"\"\"[{\"key\": \"value\"}, 81, true]\"\"\")\n[{'key': 'value'}, 81, True]\n```\n\n### Encoder options\n\n#### encode_html_chars\n\nUsed to enable special encoding of \"unsafe\" HTML characters into safer Unicode\nsequences. Default is `False`:\n\n```pycon\n>>> ujson.dumps(\"<script>John&Doe\", encode_html_chars=True)\n'\"\\\\u003cscript\\\\u003eJohn\\\\u0026Doe\"'\n```\n\n#### ensure_ascii\n\nLimits output to ASCII and escapes all extended characters above 127. Default is `True`.\nIf your end format supports UTF-8, setting this option to false is highly recommended to\nsave space:","numLines":60,"startLine":1,"totalLines":176}}}

It's designed as a drop-in replacement for Python's standard json module with better performance.\n\n## Main Module/Package\n- Import path: `import ujson`\n- Main functions: `ujson.loads()`, `ujson.dumps()`, `ujson.load()`, `ujson.dump()`\n- Implementation: C extension module (Python bindings to C code)\n\n## High-Priority Functions for Fuzzing\n\n### 1. ujson.loads()\n- **Location**: C function `JSONToObj` exposed as `ujson.loads`\n- **Purpose**: Parse JSON string into Python objects\n- **Input types**: str or bytes (JSON data)\n- **Edge cases**:\n  - Invalid JSON syntax\n  - Deeply nested structures (stack overflow)\n  - Very large numbers\n  - Unicode edge cases\n  - Malformed escape sequences\n  - Null bytes in strings\n  - Integer overflow/underflow\n  - Floating point edge cases (inf, nan)\n- **Exceptions**: ValueError (JSON decode error)\n- **Why fuzz**: C parser handling untrusted input - HIGH SECURITY IMPACT\n- **Security note**: README warns about buffer overflow vulnerabilities\n\n### 2. ujson.dumps()\n- **Location**: C function `objToJSON` exposed as `ujson.dumps`\n- **Purpose**: Serialize Python objects to JSON string\n- **Input types**: Python objects (dict, list, str, int, float, bool, None)\n- **Edge cases**:\n  - Circular references\n  - Very deep nesting\n  - Large integers\n  - Special float values (inf, -inf, nan)\n  - Unicode strings\n  - Non-serializable objects\n- **Exceptions**: TypeError, OverflowError\n- **Why fuzz**: C serialization code can have bugs\n\n### 3. ujson.load()\n- **Location**: C function reading from file\n- **Purpose**: Parse JSON from file object\n- **Input types**: file-like object\n- **Edge cases**:\n  - Same as loads() but from file\n  - Large files\n  - Encoding issues\n- **Exceptions**: ValueError, IOError\n- **Why fuzz**: File parsing adds complexity\n\n### 4. ujson.dump()\n- **Location**: C function writing to file\n- **Purpose**: Serialize to JSON file\n- **Input types**: Python object + file object\n- **Edge cases**:\n  - Same as dumps() but to file\n  - Write failures\n- **Exceptions**: TypeError, IOError\n- **Why fuzz**: File handling adds edge cases\n\n## Dependencies\n- None (pure C extension, no Python dependencies)\n- Requires C compiler to build\n\n## Known Issues\n- README WARNING: \"fundamentally ill-suited to making changes without risk of introducing new security vulnerabilities\"\n- Library is in maintenance-only mode\n- Known history of buffer overflow vulnerabilities\n- Users encouraged to migrate to orjson\n\n## Testing Strategy\n1. **PRIMARY FOCUS**: `ujson.loads()` - most critical for security\n2. **Secondary**: `ujson.dumps()` - can have edge cases\n3. Catch expected exceptions: ValueError (JSON errors)\n5. Let unexpected exceptions crash - these are BUGS or SECURITY ISSUES\n6. Focus on inputs that might cause:\n   - Buffer overflows\n   - Stack overflows (deep nesting)\n   - Integer overflows\n   - Memory corruption\n7. This is a C extension - crashes are possible and important to find!\n\n## Critical Security Note\nSince this is a C parser known to have security issues, any crashes found are VERY IMPORTANT and should be reported. Focus fuzzing effort here.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":882,"cache_creation_input_tokens":219,"cache_read_input_tokens":55939,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":219,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"10232819-9bc6-4978-aee1-98f3ecacf847","uuid":"15d954ad-9c1e-42be-b711-b6e5a2757d9e"}
